" data-portal-copyright= " Justin Tang " **28;10238;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78452883 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; They&amp;rsquo ; re manning the trenches in an increasingly difficult struggle to fend off the COVID-19 pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Canada&amp;rsquo ; s emergency-department doctors are often left in the dark about how exactly to confront the challenge , receiving conflicting advice from government and hospital employers , some of them warned Sunday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One emergency physician has just published a starkly worded opinion piece about what he called the &amp;ldquo ; void in communication&amp;rdquo ; over how health care workers in Ontario should protect themselves and their patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A spokesman for the emergency doctors&amp;rsquo ; national group says it&amp;rsquo ; s a problem across the country &amp;mdash ; a &amp;ldquo ; desultory and tentative&amp;rdquo ; approach by government that leaves health workers and hospitals largely on their own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; ve seriously let us down , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Alan Drummond of the Canadian Association of Emergency Physicians . &amp;ldquo ; We are getting mixed messaging with regards to screening , we have had decidedly mixed messaging with respect to what&amp;rsquo ; s required for personal-protective equipment &amp;hellip ; There is confusion at the ground level with all of these things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Though patients are being encouraged to stay away from hospital emergency departments if possible until the outbreak is under control , many with symptoms of what could be the novel coronavirus are , in fact , showing up at the wards . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drummond said people have been referred to his emergency department in Perth , Ont. , by the local paramedic service and employers . Dr. Bill Cherniak of the Markham Stouffville Hospital north of Toronto says his ward has been doing 10 or more COVID-19 tests a shift . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;10268;TOOLONG ... title= " Medical staff work at a computer terminal as they prepare for the opening of the COVID-19 Assessment Centre at Brewer Park Arena in Ottawa , during a media tour on Friday , March 13 , 2020. " alt= " Medical staff work at a computer terminal as they prepare for the opening of the COVID-19 Assessment Centre at Brewer Park Arena in Ottawa , during a media tour on Friday , March 13 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Justin Tang " **28;10303;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78452884 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But physicians and nurses are receiving fuzzy signals about their approach to the disease , as fears grow that the epidemic could soon get far worse in Canada , said Dr. Blair Bigham , a fifth-year emergency-medicine resident in Hamilton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His &lt;a href= " http : **34;10333;TOOLONG ... article&lt;/a&gt; in the Medical Post says doctors are getting the run-around and &amp;ldquo ; vastly different&amp;rdquo ; advice from the authorities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Doctors all over Ontario are just totally confused about what public health wants us to do , &amp;rdquo ; said Bigham in an interview . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A physician friend in Eastern Ontario , for instance , was told she had to self-isolate at home for two weeks after returning recently from a trip to the U.S. She had an appointment to carry out a medically assisted death and was forced to find a different physician to fill in for her on the sensitive assignment , said Bigham. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But another MD friend , working in southwestern Ontario , was told after making extensive inquiries that he could , in fact , show up at work after coming back from the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At a separate hospital , the intensive-care unit said traveling physicians could return to work immediately , while the emergency department said they had to stay home , said Bigham. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Doctors in Alberta and B.C. , meanwhile , have been told they definitely have to self-quarantine at home following a foreign trip , said Cherniak. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another area of confusion surrounds the personal-protective equipment doctors and nurses wear when dealing with potential COVID-19 patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bigham said emergency doctors at a Hamilton hospital recently got a memo they interpreted to mean that if they encountered a patient who might have the virus , even if they were wearing personal-protective equipment , they had go into home isolation . Which could deplete the ranks of such health workers rapidly , he noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;10369;TOOLONG ... title= " A man enters Sunnybrook Hospital , where a patient is being treated in isolation for what Canadian health officials call the first presumptive confirmed case of novel coronavirus , in Toronto , Ontario , Canada January 26 , 2020. " alt= " A man enters Sunnybrook Hospital , where a patient is being treated in isolation for what Canadian health officials call the first presumptive confirmed case of novel coronavirus , in Toronto , Ontario , Canada January 26 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Carlos Osorio " **28;10404;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78452889 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; There has also been conflicting advice about what type of masks to wear when , for instance , administering a coronavirus test . Until a few days ago , Public Health Ontario was recommending health workers don N95 respirators &amp;ndash ; snugly fitting masks that provide optimal protection &amp;ndash ; while the Public Health Agency of Canada and other provinces said looser-fitting surgical masks were sufficient . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario recently changed its advice to mirror the others . And yet , noted Bigham , the Centers for Disease Control in the U.S. is still recommending N95 masks for health professionals dealing with possible COVID-19 patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cherniak was among numerous doctors and other medical workers who retweeted a Twitter post from Bigham about his commentary , saying &amp;ldquo ; Truer words never wrote . It&amp;rsquo ; s really a mess . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in an interview he said he believes that public-health officials are doing the best they can , and that he knows of at least one Toronto-area public-health department that is being inundated with calls from doctors and nurses seeking advice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In times like this , we need really clear leadership , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; ( But ) they&amp;rsquo ; ve got a really hard job right now . They&amp;rsquo ; re managing a once-in-a-century pandemic. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10434;TOOLONG ... draconian measures may not work : Two experts say we should prioritize those at risk from COVID-19 than to try to contain the **34;10469;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt; ... href= " https : **33;10505;TOOLONG ... of Canadians worried about a coronavirus outbreak in their cities , poll **26;10540;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10568;TOOLONG ... need to act now ' : Canada public health officer raises COVID-19 pandemic threat from low-risk to ' serious ' &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; To ... " data-portal-copyright= " Doug Mills/Pool via Reuters " **28;10722;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78449624 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is a space between about 8:10 and 9:30 p.m . Wednesday that you can measure in time . It was 80 minutes , give or take , depending on how closely you follow the news . But there is another , in many ways , more tangible , gulf between those moments . At 8:10 everything was abstract . By 9:30 it was incredibly real . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It happened in a tumble . Everything slow became fast . The news bubbled over in a white water gush. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 8:10 EST , in Oklahoma City , a team doctor ran onto a basketball court seconds before an NBA game was scheduled to begin . You can see him in one clip posted online , in a blue suit and brown loafers . He appears from off-screen in a half-panicked scramble , shuffling in off the baseline and skidding toward centre court . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Video of OKC 's team doctor rushing out on the court to stop the Jazz-Thunder game before tip-off . &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/9VLx0VoD3L " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- Hoop Vault ? ? ( @HoopsVauIt ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;10752;TOOLONG ... 12 , **33;10787;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;10822;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; For several minutes , he went back and forth , from bench to bench , to the coaches and the referees . In the footage , you can see the confusion settle in as he darts to and fro . The pregame warm-ups stretch on too long . The coaches huddle . They split . The referees gather . Then suddenly , team officials begin to herd their players off the court . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By 8:30 , the game was officially called . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Across the country , in Washington , D.C. , as the confused fans streamed out of the Oklahoma arena , the president of the United States , a former reality television star , sat down to deliver a national address . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It began at 9:01 p.m . EST . It followed , on Fox , an episode of the Masked Singer , a reality show that featured , on this night , Sarah Palin , a former Republican candidate for vice-president . The last image on screen before cutting to the White House was of &lt;a href= " https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=jDOMWK2 ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Palin , in a pink and purple bear costume , dancing and rapping&lt;/a&gt; about butts &amp;mdash ; a carnival transition for a carnival politics gone utterly mad . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;10857;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;10893;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; On screen , in the Oval Office , the president looked the closest thing he comes to grave . He squinted . His right eye nearly closed . He clutched his hands . He crossed his fingers . He twiddled his thumbs as he spoke . He has never been a natural reader , the president . Nor does he do solemn well . He thrives in improv and dark jokes . He was not a man born to calm pandemic fears . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , even by those low standards , what he delivered Wednesday fell well short of the bar . It was a bizarre and tangled speech . He stumbled over words he was obviously reading . He sucked in breath between teeth as he stuttered . He boasted about an effort that he and he alone seemed to believe has been world class . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The president announced a series of drastic new measures to combat the spread of COVID-19 Wednesday , including a travel ban for much of continental Europe . But despite reading from a teleprompter , he managed to get several elements of his own plan wrong . It was a moment of national crisis , the economy in tatters , a pandemic spiralling out of control , and the emperor not only had no clothes , he needed glasses , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As the fact checkers picked apart his speech online &amp;mdash ; he got the scope of the ban wrong ; he was wrong about insurance coverage for treatment ; he was wrong on banning goods &amp;mdash ; the news tumbled forward in impossible leaps . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;10929;TOOLONG ... title= " Actor Tom Hanks and his wife actress/singer Rita Wilson have both tested positive for coronavirus. " alt= " Actor Tom Hanks and his wife actress/singer Rita Wilson have both tested positive for coronavirus. " data-portal-copyright= " Valerie Macon/AFP via Getty Images/File " **28;10964;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78449637 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 9:14 , Tom Hanks , a man so innocent and decent he signs his tweets &amp;ldquo ; Hanx , &amp;rdquo ; announced online that he had tested positive for the virus . Soon news emerged that Rudy Gobert , a basketball player for the Utah Jazz , was positive , too . His teammates were rushed off the court in Oklahoma City . They were given gloves and masks . They were quarantined in their locker room . They stayed in the arena for most of the night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The game was called off . At 9:31 , the NBA put the entire season on hold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In less than an hour and a half , a news story , something that existed somewhere in everyone&amp;rsquo ; s mind , had become the only thing , anywhere . And by Thursday morning , the reality that COVID-19 would mean dramatic changes to life in North America , not sometime in the future , but now , had begun to sink in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NHL , following the NBA&amp;rsquo ; s lead , suspended all games indefinitely . In Ontario , the government announced that all public schools would close for two weeks after the March break . Quebec and Alberta barred large public gatherings . In Ottawa , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau , his wife showing signs of being ill , was placed in self-quarantine . In B.C. , health officials advised against all non-essential travel outside of Canada , including to the United States . What was once distant was now here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10994;TOOLONG ... COVID-19 has existed in the public imagination for months now , but always as something that was happening over there . It was a problem in China . Then it was a disaster in Iran . Even when it shut down Italy , it didn&amp;rsquo ; t feel fully real . But on Wednesday , in a mad 80 minutes of news , all of that changed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After his speech ended , and the camera panned out , the president sat at his desk blinking . For whatever reason , C-Span &lt;a href= " https : **33;11188;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;continued to broadcast&lt;/a&gt; , even after a voice called from off screen &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re clear . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At that point , the president leaned back and unbuttoned his coat . &amp;ldquo ; Okaaaay , &amp;rdquo ; he said , drawing out the vowel sound . I must however base my decision on the evidence before me. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neither the family nor the lawyers for the two sides in the personal injury lawsuit could be reached for comment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The case marks a rare judicial airing in Canada of safety concerns about a widely used vaccine , as scientists rush to produce an inoculation against the new coronavirus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To help expedite the vaccine , the federal government had actually &amp;ldquo ; indemnified&amp;rdquo ; GSK , meaning the company itself would not have paid any damages had they been awarded to Amina&amp;rsquo ; s family . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The judge repeatedly voiced sympathy over the tragic death , but said a causal link had simply not been established . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The only proven relationship between the vaccine and Amina&amp;rsquo ; s death was time . Amina died five days after receiving the vaccine , &amp;rdquo ; wrote Koehnen. &amp;ldquo ; While that inflicts horrible loss and suffering upon Amina&amp;rsquo ; s parents , the fact that someone dies five days after receiving a medication does not mean that the medication caused the death . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Amid dire warnings , the World Health Organization declared the H1N1 virus a pandemic influenza strain in 2009 &amp;mdash ; a novel infection that had spread throughout the world and seemed to pose some unique risks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Governments in Canada faced major pressure to procure the vaccine and administer it widely , but at the same time there were concerns about the safety of the product after its rapid development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Smaller-scale trials before the vaccine hit the market , and large-scale surveillance after its actual use have shown it was generally effective and safe . Some evidence suggests it increased the risk of developing narcolepsy , a neurological condition that causes extreme sleepiness. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Amina developed fever and stomach pain in the days after she got her shot , felt better , then returned from school one day complaining of foot pain and feeling generally unwell . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Soon after , her brother discovered Amina unresponsive in the bathroom . She was declared dead despite desperate attempts at CPR by her mother , a nurse . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Michael Pollanen , Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s chief forensic pathologist , performed an autopsy and concluded that Amina may have died from Sudden Arrhythmic Death Syndrome ( SADS ) &amp;ndash ; which essentially describes a fatal cardiac arrest with unknown causes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;11735;TOOLONG ... title= " Abudu Ibn Adam and May Hyacenth Abudu , parents of Aminatawalla Abudu. Canada is urging its residents to avoid &amp;ldquo ; non-essential&amp;rdquo ; travel to China because of the still-spreading new coronavirus . The U.S. government has recommended against any kind of visit to the country until the disease is contained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But what about a unique group of people with little choice in their travel plans &amp;mdash ; raging epidemic . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In fact , Canada has already put a temporary halt to deportations to Wuhan and surrounding Hubei province &amp;mdash ; the new disease&amp;rsquo ; s epicentre &amp;mdash ; &lt;p&gt; The new document accuses Huawei of using deception , college researchers operating under cover and other means to steal trade secrets from six technology companies in the U.S. , violating racketeering laws . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And it alleges that as well as trying to covertly evade U.S. sanctions barring commercial relations with Iran , it did the same in selling products to North Korea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It also charges that Huawei helped the Iranian government with surveillance technology used to monitor the 2009 pro-democracy protests in Tehran . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meng has been under arrest in Vancouver since December 2018 on the heels of a U.S. extradition request that accuses her of fraud in the Iran-sanctions case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Her detention touched off a bitter feud between the countries , with China imprisoning two Canadians and curtailing trade in what is widely seen as a tit-for-tat response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The new &amp;ldquo ; superceding&amp;rdquo ; indictment , filed in a U.S. federal court in New York , includes the earlier charges and adds to them , an apparent escalation of American pressure on Huawei amid a U.S.-China trade war and debate about the firm&amp;rsquo ; s role in next-generation wireless networks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Though she is mentioned as a defendant along with Huawei companies , none of the new charges relating to IP theft and other matters apply to Meng herself , said Peter Carr , a U.S. Justice Department spokesman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She is accused of fraud by deceiving U.S. banks about Huawei&amp;rsquo ; s close ties to another company that did business in Iran contrary to U.S. sanctions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;13670;TOOLONG ... title= " Huawei Chief Financial Officer Meng Wanzhou leaves B.C. " alt= " Huawei Chief Financial Officer Meng Wanzhou leaves B.C. Supreme Court on a lunch break during her extradition hearing at B.C. In doing so , it violated the Racketeer Influenced and Corrupt Organizations Act ( RICO ) , legislation aimed at prosecuting ongoing criminal organizations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As a consequence of its campaign to steal this technology and intellectual property , Huawei was able to drastically cut its research and development costs and associated delays , giving the company a significant and unfair competitive advantage , &amp;rdquo ; said the document . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The indictment does not mention the victims of Huawei&amp;rsquo ; s alleged thefts by name , but some of the allegations appear to stem from cases already made public . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That includes charges by California-based Cisco &amp;ndash ; referred to as company one in the indictment &amp;ndash ; that Huawei misappropriated source code and manuals for its routers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another allegation seems to connect to Motorola &amp;ndash ; &amp;ldquo ; company two&amp;rdquo ; &amp;ndash ; that a relative of Huawei founder Ren Zhengfei who worked for the American firm handed over technology to Ren&amp;rsquo ; s company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another case , involving California-based &amp;ldquo ; company six , &amp;rdquo ; alleges a complex scheme by Huawei to obtain a proprietary product that it could then reverse engineer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Huawei&amp;rsquo ; s methods , according to an internal memo cited in the indictment , included continually hiring away employees from the other firm and fomenting &amp;ldquo ; internal turmoil . &amp;rdquo ; Huawei also claimed to be interested in a joint venture with the U.S. company , and ultimately made an accord with a professor at a Chinese university . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The academic in turn asked to work with the California business , without mentioning his link to Huawei , and obtained a version of the desired product , the indictment charges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; China has repeatedly demanded Meng&amp;rsquo ; s release , and taken other apparent retaliatory action . That includes detaining former Canadian diplomat Michael Kovrig and Canadian businessman Michael Spavor on ill-defined espionage charges , and abruptly escalating a convicted Canadian drug trafficker&amp;rsquo ; s sentence from 15 years&amp;rsquo ; jail to the death penalty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; ( Updated 5:35 Feb. 13 with more detail ) &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Tom BlackwellThu , 13 Feb 2020 22:59:04 **45;13927;TOOLONG ... **32;13974;TOOLONG cares about me ' Canadian stuck in Wuhan received little help from Global Affairshttp : **33;14008;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;14043;TOOLONG ... title= " Qi Jin , a Canadian citizen stuck in Wuhan , said he feels increasingly frustrated and abandoned by the lack of information he has received from Canadian officials about his possible removal from the virus-stricken city . " data-portal-copyright= " Harjit Jaswal / Facebook " **28;15795;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78361154 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Supporters of a Progressive Conservative candidate in the last Ontario election helped run a smear campaign against a party rival using leaked police documents , with the politician&amp;rsquo ; s business partner caught on video mailing out the records , a watchdog agency has revealed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The latest report on the incident from Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Office of the Independent Police Review Director ( OIPRD ) serves up more startling details of the law-enforcement-aided scheme , including the involvement of people around Tory candidate Harjit Jaswal. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jaswal &amp;mdash ; who denies taking part in the campaign to discredit Nick Gahunia &amp;mdash ; lost the race for Brampton Centre to New Democrat Sara Singh by just 89 votes in the June 2018 election . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He had won the constituency&amp;rsquo ; s Conservative nomination after the party pulled Gahunia from the running at the last minute , citing the police documents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report says Jaswal&amp;rsquo ; s then real estate partner , Sukh Toor , admitted to mailing out the packages and knew the officer who obtained the documents . But Toor told investigators he picked up the packages from Jaswal&amp;rsquo ; s campaign office and had no idea what was in them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jaswal , who also ran in the 2014 Ontario election and is a former regional director of the PC Ontario Fund , reiterated in a brief interview this week that &amp;ldquo ; I had nothing to do with this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked if Toor could have been acting on his own , the former PC candidate said &amp;ldquo ; I have no idea what you are talking about&amp;hellip ; . You can ask Mr. Toor , not me . I can not speak on behalf of other people . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toor could not be reached for comment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A spokeswoman for Conservative Premier Doug Ford said Ford would not comment on the matter , referring questions to the PC party . Party spokesman Marcus Mattinson also said he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t comment as the matter is &amp;ldquo ; not yet settled before the courts . &amp;rdquo ; He did say that Ford expects candidates to &amp;ldquo ; act with the highest level of integrity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;15825;TOOLONG ... title= " Nick Gahunia. OIPRD says then Const . Amarjit Mann , who was on long-term medical leave , asked Det. -Const . Soon Lum to access the internal police documents for him , before they were distributed to various community members in Brampton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mann resigned from the force last month , meaning a Police Services Act charge can not proceed against him , says the report , a copy of which was obtained by the National Post . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toronto police conducted a &amp;ldquo ; full criminal investigation&amp;rdquo ; into the matter but &amp;ldquo ; based on available evidence , no criminal charges were laid , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Connie Osborne . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Any leak of information or records is a clear breach of protocol and procedures and the service takes these matters extremely seriously , &amp;rdquo ; she added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gahunia , meanwhile , has sued the Toronto force and others for $12 million , accusing them of thwarting the democratic process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Me and my family are disgusted and traumatized by this whole experience , especially when the names of people behind my take down are slowly coming out , &amp;rdquo ; he said this week about the latest revelations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gahunia , a law-school graduate , was the riding association president in Brampton Centre and believes he had the inside track on the actual nomination , while polls suggested the Conservative candidate would have had a good chance of winning the riding . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But packages containing copies of Peel Regional Police reports on two encounters he had with officers &amp;ndash ; one when he was just 18 &amp;ndash ; were mailed to community members and some media just before the nomination election in April 2018 . BlackwellTue , 07 Jan 2020 22:36:19 **45;16179;TOOLONG ... **35;16226;TOOLONG of Canadian transplant patients crowd fund to cover costs , study sayshttp : **36;16263;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;16301;TOOLONG ... title= " Hundreds of Canadians have launched crowd-funding campaigns in recent years to support their organ transplants , asking for help financing drugs , transportation and lost income -- and raising thorny ethical questions , a new study indicates . " alt= " Hundreds of Canadians have launched crowd-funding campaigns in recent years to support their organ transplants , asking for help financing drugs , transportation and lost income -- and raising thorny ethical questions , a new study indicates . " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Image " **28;16336;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78360307 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For a Victoria , B.C. , woman suffering from end-stage liver disease , there was some good news : she had been deemed a candidate to undergo transplantation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The bad news ? When an organ became available , the patient and her spouse would have to pay for a three-month stay near the transplant centre in Vancouver , one of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s most expensive cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So she set up a crowd-funding page , aiming to raise $10,000 and &amp;ldquo ; relieve this stressful financial situation for my husband and myself . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The woman , who eventually got her new kidney &amp;mdash ; and raised $6,100 &amp;mdash ; was not alone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Soliciting money for medical needs on the internet may seem like a quintessentially American phenomenon , unnecessary in a country like Canada that boasts a universal , government-paid health care system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But hundreds of Canadians have launched crowd-funding campaigns in recent years to support their organ transplants , asking for help financing drugs , transportation and lost income &amp;mdash ; and raising thorny ethical questions , &lt;a href= " https : **33;16366;TOOLONG ... new study&lt;/a&gt; indicates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The more than 400 kidney and liver campaigns on gofundme.com alone underline the financial burden that&amp;rsquo ; s imposed on transplant recipients &amp;mdash ; and not covered by medicare , note the researchers from Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s SickKids Hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s more , the informal web-based system for raising money to cover those costs produces unequal results , often unrelated to medical need , they conclude . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; People requiring a new liver earned much more than those awaiting kidney transplants , while children did better than adults , the study&amp;rsquo ; s authors found . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To win donors&amp;rsquo ; sympathy , &amp;ldquo ; campaign recipients were painted as morally worthy of donation&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; victims of medical bad luck rather than poor personal behaviour &amp;mdash ; and much-loved pillars of their community , notes the paper in the journal PLOS One . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This , of course , problematically presumes that some individuals are more worthy of medical care than others , violating norms of treating medical care as an entitlement and human right , &amp;rdquo ; the authors say . &amp;ldquo ; It is ethically problematic to shift to a system of distribution that awards individuals according to perceived moral worthiness or socioeconomic advantage . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The paper also noted that crowdfunding patients often must sacrifice considerable privacy , revealing personal medical and financial information to bolster their pitch , sometimes at the urging of funding websites. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study was prompted by what seemed to be an &amp;ldquo ; increasing trend&amp;rdquo ; toward such campaigns , mostly launched by transplant patients&amp;rsquo ; family and friends , said Samantha Anthony , a SickKids&amp;rsquo ; scientist and social worker . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The research , led by Anthony , wasn&amp;rsquo ; t meant to pass judgment on the phenomenon , just highlight some of the issues around it , she said in an email interview . As to whether government should cover the costs the campaigns try to finance , that&amp;rsquo ; s a complex question and &amp;ldquo ; up to society to decide how to distribute scarce resources , &amp;rdquo ; Anthony said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The authors looked at kidney and liver transplant patients who had used GoFundMe . They found 258 kidney cases and 171 liver patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For reasons that were not clear , liver patients received more and bigger donations on average and more Facebook shares than those waiting for a new kidney , the study found . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The campaigns were varied , but all underscored the personal ordeal that comes with such severe illness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They included one that was for an eight-year-old Alberta girl suffering acute liver and heart failure due to a rare cardiac condition . Her family asked for help to pay for &amp;ldquo ; transportation , accommodations , food , lost wages ( and ) enrichment items&amp;rdquo ; for the patient . They raised $21,040 of a $35,000 goal , the girl receiving a potentially life-saving heart transplant a year later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Charlottetown woman with kidney failure managed to raise $3,311 to pay for &amp;ldquo ; medical bills , food , accommodation , loss of wages and other related costs&amp;rdquo ; to undergo a transplant in Halifax , and help others who were part of a chain organ-donation process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most asked for money to cover the costs of attending a hospital outside of where they lived , lost income and medication not reimbursed by medicare or private insurance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16401;TOOLONG ... show Toronto doctors performing Canada 's first-ever hand and forearm **31;16436;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt; ... href= " https : **33;16469;TOOLONG ... One suggested that drugs could cost $5,000 a month . Another patient said a hospital social worker had suggested she start fundraising , warning the procedure would leave her out of pocket $10,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In an effort to boost the patient&amp;rsquo ; s perceived &amp;ldquo ; worthiness , &amp;rdquo ; the study said , about a third of campaigners described the recipient as an &amp;ldquo ; amazing person , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;ldquo ; caring&amp;rsquo ; , &amp;ldquo ; loveable , &amp;rdquo ; or &amp;ldquo ; kind . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But relatively few of the crowd-funding requests criticized the health care system for allowing patients to face financial risk . That&amp;rsquo ; s concerning , the authors argue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It encourages the public to view crowd funding as a solution to the needs of Canadians in need of kidney and liver transplants , &amp;rdquo ; they say . &amp;ldquo ; By generally ignoring the systemic causes of these needs and demonstrating discrete cases where Canadians are meeting these needs via crowd funding , donors&amp;hellip ; may be less likely to view the health system as in need of reform . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For patients like the Victoria woman , who announced last July she was closing her GoFundMe page , there is mostly just gratitude to donors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have been able to recover completely with a great amount of help from each of you , &amp;rdquo ; she wrote . &lt;/p&gt; Tom BlackwellMon , 06 Jan 2020 23:38:13 **45;16593;TOOLONG ... military wants spy ' role players ' to help train special forces on surveillance , counter-surveillancehttp : //nationalpost.com/n ... src= " https : **33;16640;TOOLONG ... title= " A Canadian flag sits on a members of Canadian forces that are leaving from CFB Trenton , in Trenton , Ont. , on October 16 , 2014. " data-portal-copyright= " THE CANADIAN PRESS/Lars Hagberg " **28;16675;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78355500 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Department of National Defence is recruiting actors for a unique role : to be participants in real-life spy-vs-spy exercises . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The ministry issued an unusual &lt;a href= " https : **33;16705;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;tender &lt;/a&gt; recently , seeking &amp;ldquo ; role players&amp;rdquo ; to help train special-forces troops on the intricacies of surveillance and counter-surveillance in challenging urban environments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &amp;ldquo ; request for supply arrangement&amp;rdquo ; opens a rare window onto some of the specialized instruction provided to Canadian commandos , in this case more within the realm of intelligence work rather than battlefield tactics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some of the role players would conduct surveillance and be targets of it without talking to the trainees , others will try to &amp;ldquo ; extract&amp;rdquo ; clues in person-to-person verbal interaction , says the tender document . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The operational environment is complex , dynamic and sometimes in unfamiliar international urban areas , &amp;rdquo ; the tender indicates . &amp;ldquo ; This requires realistic training scenarios to prepare the operators for the challenges they are likely to face . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16740;TOOLONG ... of rummaging colleagues , spies and foreign visitors : unique FBI document warns academia about China **27;16775;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16804;TOOLONG ... and clear ' threat : What Canada 's spy chief says about China behind closed **26;16839;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... The military employs contractors for the work partly to ensure a more authentic experience . Compared to other military personnel , outside role players are less likely to be identified by the troops undergoing training , said Sue Beler , a spokeswoman for Canadian Special Operations Forces Command ( CANSOFCOM ) . The command&amp;rsquo ; s main units are the secretive Joint Task Force Two ( JTF2 ) and Canadian Special Operations Regiment , plus the 427 Special Operations Aviation Squadron and the Joint Incident Response Unit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The surveillance training is run out of the Dwyer Hill Training Centre near Ottawa , home to the JTF2 , but the instruction is not for any specific type of special forces members , said Beler. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The exercises are designed to prepare personnel for missions in overseas cities and to improve their ability to work &amp;ldquo ; with enhanced situational awareness and a reduced profile , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; National Defence releases little information about the elite special-operations units , though it is known they have served in world hotspots ranging from the Balkans in the 1990s to Afghanistan after 9/11 and present-day Iraq , where they recently taught Kurdish soldiers fighting ISIL. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gen. Mike Rouleau , then CANSOFCOM&amp;rsquo ; s commanding officer , hinted at the need for surveillance training in a 2016 Toronto Star interview , saying that not all the special forces&amp;rsquo ; work involves &amp;ldquo ; gunfire . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is sending smart people into complex areas and being able to provide ground truth or information back so senior leadership can make better informed decisions , &amp;rdquo ; Rouleau told the Star . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The tender calls for up to eight &amp;ldquo ; non-verbal&amp;rdquo ; role players for each training event who will conduct surveillance and be targets of surveillance both in vehicles and on foot . As many as another eight &amp;ldquo ; verbal&amp;rdquo ; players per event will interact with the soldiers to &amp;ldquo ; extract or provide information cues. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The exercises are slated to occur in cities across Canada , said Beler. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The document requests &amp;ldquo ; qualified&amp;rdquo ; role players , but U.S. defence contractors who have &lt;a href= " https : **33;16867;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;recently advertised&lt;/a&gt; for similar services are more specific about their needs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of them , North Carolina-based Telum Protection Corp. , &lt;a href= " https : **33;16902;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;requires its role players&lt;/a&gt; to hold &amp;ldquo ; active secret clearance&amp;rdquo ; and have undergone a counterintelligence polygraph test , among other prerequisites . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company , which provides training to intelligence and special-forces personnel in the U.S. military , hires experts in surveillance , who sometimes represent specific cultures or languages , said Alfredo R. Quiros , Telum&amp;rsquo ; s CEO . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What you&amp;rsquo ; re looking for is to provide realism to the training , &amp;rdquo ; he said in an interview . &amp;ldquo ; Ultimately , it will better prepare your men and women for mission success and &amp;mdash ; here is the key word &amp;mdash ; survivability. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not all of the training involves cloak-and-dagger stuff ; it could be about conducting a meeting with a source at a restaurant without drawing attention , or simply briefing a diplomat , said Quiros. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You have a young captain who goes through all this training , and the first thing they have to do when they go to a foreign country is go to the embassy and they have to talk to the ambassador , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; Why not include in the training , how to talk to an ambassador ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; Tom BlackwellThu , 02 Jan 2020 21:52:50 **45;16937;TOOLONG ... with Beijing ties looks to bring face-recognition tech to Canadian storeshttp : **34;16984;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;17020;TOOLONG ... title= " The payment systems capture an image of the shopper 's face which is then linked to his or her account . " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Redmond / AFP " **28;19844;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78282348 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Canadian consular officials visited Michael Spavor in a Chinese prison on Monday &amp;mdash ; and Michael Kovrig last Friday &amp;mdash ; they were led by none other than the Beijing embassy&amp;rsquo ; s top official . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Newly minted ambassador Dominic Barton saw first-hand , a government source said , what&amp;rsquo ; s at stake in arguably this country&amp;rsquo ; s toughest diplomatic assignment in a generation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now Barton just has to figure out how to end the pair&amp;rsquo ; s ordeal , almost a year after they were detained on nebulous espionage charges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two of his predecessors and two academic experts interviewed this week differ on the specifics of tackling that and other challenges , including billions in Canadian exports blocked by Chinese action . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But they agreed on one point : with a newly re-elected Liberal government and a new ambassador , Ottawa must overhaul its whole strategy for resolving the country&amp;rsquo ; s bitter quarrel with China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Because whatever it&amp;rsquo ; s done so far hasn&amp;rsquo ; t worked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Central to that revamp is recognizing that a return to the earlier &amp;ldquo ; romantic&amp;rdquo ; era of enthusiastic engagement with China is not in the cards , the experts concurred . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The only language China understands is one of firmness , &amp;rdquo ; said Guy Saint-Jacques , who was the Beijing ambassador from 2012 to 2016. &amp;ldquo ; Now that we have seen the dark side of China and how they can punish you if you do something they don&amp;rsquo ; t like or if you don&amp;rsquo ; t obey their **28;19874;TOOLONG , I think it&amp;rsquo ; s appropriate for us to reassess our engagement strategy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A new strategy could include giving the boot to interfering Chinese diplomats here and cracking down on Fentanyl imports , boycotting the 2022 winter Olympics or , on the contrary , actually toning down the relatively mild rhetoric Canada has directed at Beijing in public , the experts said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Already , though , there is doubt about whether a change in approach is likely . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The appointment of the &amp;ldquo ; incredibly talented&amp;rdquo ; Barton as ambassador suggests the Liberals are anxious only to turn the clock back to old ways of interacting with Beijing , said David Mulroney , ambassador from 2009 to 2012 . Barton is a former China boss for consulting company McKinsey and widely praised as a brilliant business mind . But he worked closely with powerful Chinese business people and politicians , for a firm Mulroney says is &amp;ldquo ; almost sycophantic&amp;rdquo ; toward the Communist leadership . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I really wonder whether someone who is on the record as saying he&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;lsquo ; bullish&amp;rsquo ; about China&amp;rsquo ; s strongman leader is really the right person to interpret China for the government of Canada , &amp;rdquo ; said the ex-ambassador. &amp;ldquo ; What we need is a revised China policy , one that says we are not going to relentlessly build the relationship . In fact , that&amp;rsquo ; s actually a little dangerous for us. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The China impasse began last December when Canada arrested Meng Wanzhou , the Huawei Technologies chief financial officer and daughter of its founder , on an extradition request from the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In what are widely seen as tit-for-tat measures , China arrested Spavor , a businessman who organized cultural visits to North Korea , and Kovrig , a former official in the Beijing embassy working for a peace-focused NGO . Not long after , Chinese courts abruptly imposed a death sentence on Canadian Robert Schellenberg , who had earlier been sentenced to 15 years for drug trafficking . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then Canadian exports of canola , pork and other agricultural products were stopped on questionable safety grounds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;19904;TOOLONG ... title= " Turnisa Matsedik-Qira , of the Vancouver Uyghur Association , demonstrates against China 's treatment of Uyghurs while holding a photo of detained Canadians Michael Spavor ( L ) and Michael Kovrig outside a court appearance for Huawei Chief Financial Officer , Meng Wanzhou at the British Columbia Supreme Court in Vancouver on May 8 , 2019. " alt= " Turnisa Matsedik-Qira , of the Vancouver Uyghur Association , demonstrates against China 's treatment of Uyghurs while holding a photo of detained Canadians Michael Spavor ( L ) and Michael Kovrig outside a court appearance for Huawei Chief Financial Officer , Meng Wanzhou at the British Columbia Supreme Court in Vancouver on May 8 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Redmond / AFP " **28;19939;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78282385 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government responded by criticizing all those actions , and by recruiting several other countries to voice their displeasure at the detention of the &amp;ldquo ; two Michaels , &amp;rdquo ; a widely praised tactic . Ottawa also filed a complaint with the World Trade Organization over the canola issue , but Trudeau said in September he wanted to avoid escalating the spat any further . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nothing seems to have budged on the Chinese side , as Beijing continues to insist that Canada release Meng , even as a court in B.C. methodically vets the extradition request . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Canadian government&amp;rsquo ; s policy since December of last year has not delivered the desired result , &amp;rdquo ; said Charles Burton , a former diplomat in Beijing and professor at Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Brock University . &amp;ldquo ; In fact , our lack of response I think is taken by the Chinese authorities as passive consent for their gross violations of normal diplomatic practice . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada should make it clear it&amp;rsquo ; s no longer business as usual , he said . That could mean ending all parliamentary exchanges , boycotting the Olympics in China in two years &amp;ndash ; if the Canadians are still detained then &amp;ndash ; taking swift action to expel diplomats found to have improperly exerted influence or intimidation in Canada , and aggressively combating money laundering by Chinese citizens and others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saint-Jacques advocated working harder with allies to bolster multi-lateral systems and push China to follow rules on issues such as intellectual property . He also suggested reviewing whether Canadian university researchers should continue co-operating with Chinese delegations in key areas like artificial intelligence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada doesn&amp;rsquo ; t need to provoke Beijing , but also shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t &amp;ldquo ; pretend that China is our friend&amp;rdquo ; and try constantly to curry favour , said Mulroney . He suggests adopting a more confident posture that recognizes Canadian exports will always be needed by China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19969;TOOLONG ... Harjit Sajjan , defence minister , &lt;a href= " https : **33;21940;TOOLONG ... at a celebration in Vancouver&lt;/a&gt; , though said he did so as a candidate and didn&amp;rsquo ; t stay long . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Such events have been held in past years , and many municipalities allow community groups to hold flag-raising parties at city halls on the national day of their countries of origin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the China events have been particularly contentious this year in the face of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s bitter diplomatic feud with Beijing , the mass protests in Hong Kong and growing concern about human-rights abuses in China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two Canadians have been imprisoned by Chinese authorities on ill-defined espionage charges in what is widely considered a tit-for-tat response to Meng&amp;rsquo ; s arrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; While our Canadian citizens have been bullied , while our country has been bullied by this totalitarian regime , why should we still continue with this kind of celebratory event ? &amp;rdquo ; asked Gloria Fung , head of the group Canada-Hong Kong Link . &amp;ldquo ; Our politicians need to show a higher level of political wisdom , to understand what not to do on Canadian soil . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before this weekend , flag raising ceremonies to commemorate the 70th anniversary had already been held in at least two Ontario cities , as well as at the Ontario legislature , and locations in B.C .. At Toronto City Hall , Mayor John Tory refused to take part , citing tensions between the two countries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Announcements of the weekend events suggested they were jointly organized by each city and the Federation of Chinese Canadians of Markham or a sister group &amp;mdash ; both headed by Dr. Ken Ng &amp;mdash ; and were sponsored by the TD Bank and the Chinese consulate general in Toronto . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The flurry of commemorations did not sit well with those troubled by Beijing&amp;rsquo ; s increasing grip on Hong Kong , prompting many to email the cities&amp;rsquo ; mayors in protest , said a spokesman for the group Torontonians Stand with Hong Kong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A lot of Hong Kong Canadians , when they found out there were three of them , people got furious , &amp;rdquo ; said the spokesman , who asked only to be quoted by his first name , John , because of safety concerns related to his advocacy . &amp;ldquo ; At a time like this , where two Canadians have been detained and our relationship with China is at a very low point , is it really necessary to have all these celebrations ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ken Ng could not be reached for comment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in emails and Facebook messages , he said the raising of the Chinese flag on China&amp;rsquo ; s national day has taken place since 2014 to &amp;ldquo ; recognize the contributions and the heritage of people of Chinese descent in the region . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ng also noted that China is now Canada&amp;rsquo ; s second biggest trading partner and the two countries have had diplomatic relations for 50 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even so , his organization decided late last week to shelve the events in Vaughan and Richmond Hill . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are respecting the fact that there are currently some unsolved issues with the Canada-China relationship , and have decided to forgo this year&amp;rsquo ; s flag raising , &amp;rdquo ; Ng wrote in an email to Richmond Hill city council last Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;21975;TOOLONG ... How do you get college students to eat less trash ? " data-portal-copyright= " Supplied " **28;22435;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78246549 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For 10 weeks , five days a week , four hours a day , three years ago , Matheus Mistura , a decorated grad student with a glittering academic career , stood next to a hot table in a cramped cafeteria peeping at students&amp;rsquo ; plates . On cold days and on hot , on rainy days and clear days and days when he&amp;rsquo ; d rather be doing anything , literally anything , other than looking at other people&amp;rsquo ; s food , Mistura manned his post . &amp;ldquo ; Fridays were tough , &amp;rdquo ; he said . But he had to do it , on Fridays and every other day too , or the data wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t match . So there he went , day after day , snooping at plates and recording what he saw . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the time , Mistura was a graduate student in kinesiology , with a focus on behavioural economics and nudge theory , at the University of Victoria . He was watching to see how many students took a vegetable from the hot entr&amp;eacute ; e station at the school&amp;rsquo ; s aging residence cafeteria . His brute manual observation was key to a study aimed at discovering whether behavioural &amp;ldquo ; nudges&amp;rdquo ; could be used to push some the world&amp;rsquo ; s least responsible eaters into making better choices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For two stretches in the fall term that year , Mistura , under the supervision of Patti-Jean Naylor , an associate professor in the University of Victoria School of Exercise Science , Physical and Health Education , made subtle alterations to the food environment at the hot station . With the co-operation of the food services staff , he had a fresh , raw vegetable option added to the normal cooked vegetable sides that appeared daily in the steam tray . Mistura also added two visual cues to the environment , each designed to prime the minds of the junk-seeking , mostly teenage students . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Above the fresh vegetables , down the row from the hot entr&amp;eacute ; es &amp;mdash ; lasagna , chicken in mushroom sauce , things of that nature &amp;mdash ; Mistura placed two signs . One had a smiling green apple in a red headband flexing its very un-apple like biceps between the words &amp;ldquo ; FRESH vegetables&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; Be healthy ! &amp;rdquo ; The other had a stalk of broccoli in black sunglasses &amp;mdash ; a cool broccoli , the kind of broccoli who knows a guy who can hook you up &amp;mdash ; giving a thumb&amp;rsquo ; s up beneath a message , in green letters , that read : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; FRESH VEGGIES &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Health at a great price &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; -$2- &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;22465;TOOLONG ... title= " Students are eating a fraction of their required fruit and veggies , research shows . " alt= " Students are eating a fraction of their required fruit and veggies , research shows . " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images " **28;22500;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78246576 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For two weeks before the intervention , Mistura , aided occasionally by a research assistant , recorded both the number of students eating from the hot table during the lunch and dinner rushes and the subsection of those students that selected a hot vegetable . He then repeated the same process with the fresh vegetable option added in , along with the signs , counting both the total number of eaters and the number of vegetable buyers , both hot and cold . He then did the whole process over again , control and intervention , from scratch . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The goal was to see whether the nudges &amp;mdash ; the fresh , attractive vegetables , usually carrots and celery &amp;mdash ; and the posters led to a measurable uptick in vegetable selection . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The experiment was one of a series undertaken by Naylor&amp;rsquo ; s grad students aimed at improving the dietary choices of university students . The myth of the freshman 15 ( pounds put on ) isn&amp;rsquo ; t quite true , Naylor said , but it is grounded in reality . University students who are away from home for the first time and living in residence do tend to put on between 3.5 and 7.5 pounds in their first year . &amp;ldquo ; They leave home . It&amp;rsquo ; s the first time they&amp;rsquo ; re making decisions on their own and they are in a very complex food environment , &amp;rdquo ; Naylor said . &amp;ldquo ; So it&amp;rsquo ; s logical . And yes , there can be some weight gain . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22530;TOOLONG ... can not be what you can not see ' : Chef 's Table star Asma Khan dishes on time-honoured Indian recipes and turning opportunity into **29;22565;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22596;TOOLONG ... do food festivals always seem to turn into unmitigated disasters ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt; ... href= " https : **33;22631;TOOLONG ... beef is n't bad for you : Scientists conclude there is no need to eat less red or processed **25;22666;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &g; ... From a health science point of view the weight isn&amp;rsquo ; t the issue , Naylor said , overall health is . But fruit and vegetable consumption is a key indicator of both and research shows that university-age students are eating just a fraction of the fruits and veggies they need . Naylor&amp;rsquo ; s graduate students have been looking at ways to use behavioural economics to change that . &amp;ldquo ; This is the science of using nudging for public health aims , &amp;rdquo ; Naylor said . &amp;ldquo ; But marketers have used it for years . Some of it just through intuition and doing it . Now , there&amp;rsquo ; s quite a bit of science . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In one experiment , one of Naylor&amp;rsquo ; s students sent a test group of off-campus students home with special plates designed to subtly prod them toward healthier eating . The images on the plate mimic the rough proportions of a healthy diet . About half the plate features a broccoli forest above blueberries with the message &amp;ldquo ; eat as many fruits and veggies as you&amp;rsquo ; d like ! ! ! &amp;rdquo ; Below the broccoli are smaller pictures of a fish ( protein ) and slice of bread ( whole grains ) along with a tiny dot for fat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The results of that study have yet to be published in a peer-reviewed journal , but Naylor said they were quite promising . The students reported a measurable increase in both fruit and vegetable consumption after taking home the plates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The results of the cafeteria study , published in August in the journal Nutrients , were a little more mixed . In the first intervention , Mistura recorded a small , but statistically significant increase in vegetable consumption . But when he did the experiment over again a second time , that increase effectively disappeared . &amp;ldquo ; I think I was cautiously optimistic that the nudge probably worked over a very short term , somewhat , &amp;rdquo ; Naylor said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The experiment took place in the real world , Naylor stressed . It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t a test cafeteria or a lab . And in the real world , &amp;ldquo ; you get a lot of real world problems with evaluation . &amp;rdquo ; For one thing , the nudge only focussed on a single element &amp;mdash ; the hot table &amp;mdash ; in a larger cafeteria . It was competing with a host of other behavioural cues , some deliberate , like signs , and others purely environmental , like the smell of French fries or sizzling bacon . &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re focussing on fruit and veg , &amp;rdquo ; Naylor said . &amp;ldquo ; But you have to remember that&amp;rsquo ; s there&amp;rsquo ; s 10 to 15 nudges going on . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mistura , who is now a clinical research co-ordinator at the University of Ottawa Heart Institute , doesn&amp;rsquo ; t regret the weeks and weeks he put in in the cafeteria , standing 2.5 metres from the hot station , watching what the teenagers ate . Nudges are cheap , he believes . Even if they only make a small difference they can be well worth the price . Still , he did express a little bit of disappointment with the results . &amp;ldquo ; I was expecting a little bit more difference , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I thought the students would be a little more excited . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **32;22693;TOOLONG " **39;22727;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Richard WarnicaThu , 03 Oct 2019 18:50:22 **45;22768;TOOLONG ... were living the dream . " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images " **28;22884;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78234500 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In some ways , the couple represented the epitome of success . A few years after arriving in Canada from China , they founded steel companies back in Guangzhou now worth over $70 million , sunk millions into a charitable trust and funded a lavish lifestyle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That includes an $8-million house and $7-million condo in Vancouver , two aircraft and hundreds of thousands of dollars in jewelry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But then the marriage &amp;mdash ; and their dream life &amp;mdash ; started to crumble , the husband and wife descending into &amp;ldquo ; unrestrained warfare , &amp;rdquo ; a B.C. Supreme Court judge observed this month in an eyebrow-raising &lt;a href= " https : **33;22914;TOOLONG ... ruling&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The wife once threw a cup at her husband , the cut needing stitches to fix ; he broke into the family home with a hammer after being kicked out , later trashed their apartment in China with an axe and threatened to strangle his teenage daughter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The husband also insisted that having mistresses was his due &amp;mdash ; and culturally appropriate &amp;mdash ; then bought his wife a $1-million diamond ring to try to win her back . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The acrimony even continued at trial itself , which was interrupted with &amp;ldquo ; a few angry , abusive and intemperate outbursts&amp;rdquo ; by the husband . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both sides exit the marriage still very rich . But the years of turmoil , family violence and &amp;ldquo ; prolific litigation&amp;rdquo ; have taken their toll , leaving the couple and their three children &amp;ldquo ; deeply hurt by it in various ways , &amp;rdquo ; said Justice Warren Milman. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The family are identified in the judgment only by initials under a court-ordered publication ban . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As well as essentially dividing the couple&amp;rsquo ; s China business assets down the middle , Milman ordered the husband , Y.Z. , to stay away from his wife and children , except when one of the children consents to a meeting with an adult chaperone present . The daughters , aged 16 and 22 , feel they would be at risk without a protection order , the judge said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Milman said he is not without sympathy for Y.Z. He felt &amp;ldquo ; sincere and heartfelt&amp;rdquo ; pain at becoming estranged from his daughters , and his &amp;ldquo ; harassment&amp;rdquo ; of them was often a genuine if misguided effort to express his feelings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Y.Z. needs to acknowledge his role in the dismal situation , and respect his children&amp;rsquo ; s boundaries , said the judge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lawyers for both sides said Monday they could not comment on the case , including whether appeals are possible . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Y.Z. &amp;mdash ; an engineer at a steel company &amp;mdash ; and his wife A.C. &amp;mdash ; a French-literature graduate &amp;mdash ; met in China but moved to Canada in 1997 along with his daughter , M.Z. , from a previous marriage , eventually becoming Canadian citizens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He started a steel-wool manufacturing plant in the southern-China city of Guangzhou in 2001 and it rapidly became a success , allowing the family to move into a home in Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s posh Shaughnessy neighbourhood . But there were already tensions , over M.Z. , who eventually was sent to a boarding school . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When their younger children became teenagers , Y.Z.&amp;lsquo ; s relationship with them deteriorated as well . One said that was partly because of evidence they found of his affairs with younger women in China and his &amp;ldquo ; dictatorial&amp;rdquo ; manner , Milman wrote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Violence sometimes erupted . The wife later apologized for throwing the cup , which she said was not aimed at him , though later also launched a cell phone at her husband , &amp;ldquo ; this time missing the mark . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the family business , a quarrel led to Y.Z. punching his wife , which in turn prompted her cousin , a &amp;ldquo ; burly individual , &amp;rdquo ; to chase him into the company cafeteria . The cousin tackled the husband and &amp;ldquo ; A.C. proceeded to slap him in front of a number of astonished employees , &amp;rdquo ; said the judge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This incident led Y.Z. to hire his own private security guards as the war at work continued to escalate . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22949;TOOLONG ... divorce : Why are more baby boomers ending their marriages when they get older ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22984;TOOLONG ... neurosurgeon who murdered wife after she filed for divorce faces disciplinary **28;23019;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23049;TOOLONG ... are not killing -- they 're saving marriage in the U.S. by not getting divorced like baby **28;23084;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... With the husband asserting that his business success gave him the right to have mistresses &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; which he described as being more culturally acceptable in the PRC than may be the case in North America&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; his wife left , and took up herself with a music teacher . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The husband&amp;rsquo ; s attempts to win back A.C. included buying her a diamond ring valued at $940,000 , but it was to no avail . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On one occasion he became enraged on hearing that his wife had discovered an incoming text she believed was from one of his lovers on a computer he had left at the family home . " data-portal-copyright= " Handout / Twitter " **28;23231;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78222080 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another federal election candidate&amp;rsquo ; s social-media past has come back to haunt him , prompting an abject apology for allegedly homophobic comments and calls from his main rival to resign . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative Arpan Khanna was scheduled to campaign Friday evening in Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Brampton North riding with Tory leader Andrew Scheer , but Friday morning was in damage-control mode . Khanna issued a statement voicing deep regret over comments he made on Twitter nine years ago , at a time he said he was a teenager . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just a day into the election campaign , Khanna is at least the third candidate to be tripped up by online history , with a Liberal and a Conservative already forced to resign over past remarks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to &lt;a href= " https : **33;23261;TOOLONG ... copy of the 2010 tweet&lt;/a&gt; posted by Liberal MP Ruby Sahota , he wrote in response to someone else&amp;rsquo ; s tweet about a college admission : &amp;ldquo ; me and haroon are going to f&amp;mdash ; your guys shit up &amp;hellip ; if that fag stays in jackswayy. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From what Sahota posted , it&amp;rsquo ; s not clear the context of the comment or whom he was referring to as a &amp;ldquo ; fag , &amp;rdquo ; but the word is widely considered to be a highly derogatory term for gay people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Khanna , a former Conservative backroom organizer and law school graduate , responded within an hour . Only a day earlier , another Conservative candidate had resigned because of &amp;ldquo ; discriminatory&amp;rdquo ; online comments about Islam and certain ethnic groups . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I deeply regret the offensive language I used when I was a teenager , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;23296;TOOLONG ... wrote on Twitter&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;ldquo ; I have come to understand that creating safer and more inclusive spaces LGBTQ+ people in Canada happens in our homes , workplaces , on social media , and in the conversations we have every day . I apologize unequivocally. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The candidate&amp;rsquo ; s Linked-In page indicates he was in the midst of an undergraduate degree at Western University and was vice president of &amp;ldquo ; election readiness&amp;rdquo ; for the Western Conservatives at the time of the Tweet. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He began working a year later as a &amp;ldquo ; community outreach advisor&amp;rdquo ; for the Ontario Tories , and went on to fill various roles with the provincial party and the Harper government . " data-portal-copyright= " GREG BAKER/AFP/Getty Images " **28;25299;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78170715 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unidentified foreign powers are using &amp;ldquo ; a range of traditional and non-traditional intelligence collection tradecraft&amp;rdquo ; to try to acquire Canadian technology and expertise , which could result in lost jobs and competitiveness , said Tahera Mufti , a spokeswoman for the Canadian Security Intelligence Service . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; CSIS routinely engages with a variety of stakeholders , including in the private sector and universities , to advise them of potential threats , &amp;rdquo ; she added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mufti pointed to a five-paragraph section on economic espionage in the service&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;25329;TOOLONG ... annual report&lt;/a&gt; , and a &lt;a href= " https : **33;25364;TOOLONG ... director David Vigneault last year , warning business leaders about the threat in generic terms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; U.S. authorities , on the other hand , have made clear they consider improper siphoning of secrets and other advanced knowledge from research facilities by China specifically to be a major problem . FBI director Christopher Wray controversially pointed the finger last year at students and professors of Chinese background , suggesting a &amp;ldquo ; whole of society&amp;rdquo ; response to the threat is needed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several people have been charged in connection with such activity , though critics have accused the government of racial profiling and paranoia in the area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;25399;TOOLONG ... The Risk to Academia document takes some pains to acknowledge that immigrants and visiting students and scholars are integral to the U.S. research system , saying the &amp;ldquo ; vast majority&amp;rdquo ; of those from China are in the States for legitimate academic reasons . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it lays out in detail the Chinese government&amp;rsquo ; s tactics to vacuum up intellectual property &amp;mdash ; such as using some students and professors as &amp;ldquo ; non-traditional collectors&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; and suggests colleges and other research organizations should play a part in countering those tactics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The document warns that &amp;ldquo ; foreign adversaries&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; its title singling out adversaries from China &amp;mdash ; could elicit information by &amp;ldquo ; using flattery , assuming knowledge , asking leading questions &amp;hellip ; or feigning ignorance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Intelligence agents will look to exploit vulnerabilities , keying in on ideology or loyalty to a country other than the U.S. ; greed or financial stress ; ego or self-image and &amp;ldquo ; anger , revenge or disaffection , &amp;rdquo ; the FBI advises . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They can spend years targeting an individual and developing a relationship that leads the student , professor or researcher &amp;mdash ; either wittingly or unwittingly &amp;mdash ; to provide information to the foreign adversary . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The briefing suggests ways to detect &amp;ldquo ; insider threats&amp;rdquo ; unexplained affluence , &amp;rdquo ; the briefing says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its advice on how to prevent espionage , the FBI notes that campus visits by foreign researchers can be risky . &lt;/p&gt; Critics feared the legislation could be used to dispatch enemies of Beijing to a legal system controlled by the Communist Party . Network " **28;27217;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78128393 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Liberal MP hired his girlfriend as a constituency assistant , then fired her at his wife&amp;rsquo ; s behest and refused to provide child support to their daughter , according to allegations contained in two lawyers&amp;rsquo ; letters obtained by the National Post . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ying ( Stella ) Yu &amp;mdash ; who says she gave up a lucrative career in business to work for Don Valley North MP Geng Tan &amp;mdash ; fell into deep depression after her &amp;ldquo ; ignominious&amp;rdquo ; dismissal , with suicide attempts , anxiety attacks and crying fits , the two letters claim . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the MP dismissed Yu&amp;rsquo ; s mental-health problems , one of the letters alleges , urging her not to get professional help . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tan , 55 , has strongly denied the allegations , saying Yu&amp;rsquo ; s job in his constituency office was supposed to be temporary , and that he had only provided her a donation of sperm without agreeing to support the resulting child . There was no &amp;ldquo ; misuse&amp;rdquo ; of their acquaintance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I will strongly fight for my dignity and honour which is improperly being maligned , alleging wrong facts and concealing the real ones , &amp;rdquo ; the MP said in a statement to the Post . &amp;ldquo ; I have strong support and love of my family . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He promised to bring a &amp;ldquo ; strong legal response . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The accusations have surfaced days after Tan &amp;mdash ; who made history in 2015 as the first Mandarin-speaking , mainland-China native to be elected to Parliament &amp;mdash ; unexpectedly announced that he would not run for re-election in October . In recent days Tan and his wife have issued statements describing him as a devoted family man . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yu , 54 , acknowledged that Tan provided her with a sperm donation for use in in-vitro fertilization , but said it was merely because she had difficulty getting pregnant naturally at her age . She said her relationship with the MP ended last September , but that she held off on court action in the hope he would voluntarily provide child support and play a part in their daughter&amp;rsquo ; s life . When that did not happen , she said , she decided reluctantly to make her allegations openly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Even now , I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to hurt him , but I have no other choice ( but to go public ) , &amp;rdquo ; Yu said in an interview with the Post . &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t have any hope . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tan surprised the political world when he won the Liberal nomination for Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Don Valley North riding before the past election , defeating a candidate favoured by Prime Minister Justin Trudeau . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He then won the constituency with 51 per cent of the popular vote . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a Facebook post last December , Tan announced that he had been re-nominated for this year&amp;rsquo ; s election , thanking Innovation Minister Navdeep Bains for his words of support . &amp;ldquo ; We have accomplished a lot together so far , &amp;rdquo ; Tan wrote . &amp;ldquo ; But there is still a lot more to do as we head into 2019 and beyond . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then on June 23 , he revealed in another post that he would not be running , saying &amp;ldquo ; time has now come to spend more time with family and pursue other careers . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Geng , you are a great MP , a loving and caring father and a good husband , &amp;rdquo ; wife Laura Huang commented on his Facebook page . &amp;ldquo ; As your wife , I firmly support you everything you did and will do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chinese-language media had already reported &amp;ldquo ; rumours&amp;rdquo ; of an affair , suggesting that Tan should address the subject publicly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But long before recent developments , lawyer Lai-King Hum sent Tan a letter alleging a surprising series of events , saying that Yu had sought legal help on the advice of mental-health professionals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The six-page , May 2017 missive said Yu gave up a career in finance and real estate that earned her over $200,000 a year to take a $48,000-a-year job as a constituency assistant in December 2015 . She had earlier worked as a volunteer on his campaign , drumming up support in the Chinese-Canadian community and raising money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tan and his constituency office manager praised her work , but five months later in April 2016 she was &amp;ldquo ; ignominiously terminated , &amp;rdquo ; wrote Hum . The MP said his wife &amp;ldquo ; did not want her in the office , and that there was no room for any negotiation , &amp;rdquo ; alleged the lawyer . Huang herself confirmed the next day that it was her decision , the letter said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yu descended into depression and repeatedly attempted suicide in May and June of 2016 , the lawyer charged . But Tan &amp;ldquo ; discouraged her from seeking any assistance from a psychologist or social worker , &amp;rdquo ; suggesting she was suffering from menopause , the lawyer alleged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their personal relationship began in 2013 and the MP had repeatedly promised that he would leave his wife and make a life with her , the letter alleged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But rather than threaten legal action , Hum suggested seeking an &amp;ldquo ; amicable resolution , &amp;rdquo ; and urged Tan to &amp;ldquo ; consider your legal obligations , as well as see that compassion is required in this situation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tan , however , told the Post that Yu&amp;rsquo ; s job &amp;ldquo ; was for a short duration , and during her employment she has been treated properly and fairly .
